Symptom fixes are failure.\n\n**Violating the letter of this process is violating the spirit of debugging.**\n\n## The Iron Law\n\n```\nNO FIXES WITHOUT ROOT CAUSE INVESTIGATION FIRST\n```\n\nIf you haven't completed Phase 1, you cannot propose fixes.\n\n## When to Use\n\nUse for ANY technical issue:\n- Test failures\n- Bugs in production\n- Unexpected behavior\n- Performance problems\n- Build failures\n- Integration issues\n\n**Use this ESPECIALLY when:**\n- Under time pressure (emergencies make guessing tempting)\n- \"Just one quick fix\" seems obvious\n- You've already tried multiple fixes\n- Previous fix didn't work\n- You don't fully understand the issue\n\n**Don't skip when:**\n- Issue seems simple (simple bugs have root causes too)\n- You're in a hurry (rushing guarantees rework)\n- Manager wants it fixed NOW (systematic is faster than thrashing)\n\n## The Four Phases\n\nYou MUST complete each phase before proceeding to the next.\n\n### Phase 1: Root Cause Investigation\n\n**BEFORE attempting ANY fix:**\n\n1. **Read Error Messages Carefully**\n   - Don't skip past errors or warnings\n   - They often contain the exact solution\n   - Read stack traces completely\n   - Note line numbers, file paths, error codes\n\n2. **Reproduce Consistently**\n   - Can you trigger it reliably?\n   - What are the exact steps?\n   - Does it happen every time?\n   - If not reproducible → gather more data, don't guess\n\n3. **Check Recent Changes**\n   - What changed that could cause this?\n   - Git diff, recent commits\n   - New dependencies, config changes\n   - Environmental differences\n\n4. **Gather Evidence in Multi-Component Systems**\n\n   **WHEN system has multiple components (CI → build → signing, API → service → database):**\n\n   **BEFORE proposing fixes, add diagnostic instrumentation:**\n   ```\n   For EACH component boundary:\n     - Log what data enters component\n     - Log what data exits component\n     - Verify environment/config propagation\n     - Check state at each layer\n\n   Run once to gather evidence showing WHERE it breaks\n   THEN analyze evidence to identify failing component\n   THEN investigate that specific component\n   ```\n\n   **Example (multi-layer system):**\n   ```bash\n   # Layer 1: Workflow\n   echo \"=== Secrets available in workflow: ===\"\n   echo \"IDENTITY: ${IDENTITY:+SET}${IDENTITY:-UNSET}\"\n\n   # Layer 2: Build script\n   echo \"=== Env vars in build script: ===\"\n   env | grep IDENTITY || echo \"IDENTITY not in environment\"\n\n   # Layer 3: Signing script\n   echo \"=== Keychain state: ===\"\n   security list-keychains\n   security find-identity -v\n\n   # Layer 4: Actual signing\n   codesign --sign \"$IDENTITY\" --verbose=4 \"$APP\"\n   ```\n\n   **This reveals:** Which layer fails (secrets → workflow ✓, workflow → build ✗)\n\n5. **Trace Data Flow**\n\n   **WHEN error is deep in call stack:**\n\n   See `root-cause-tracing.md` in this directory for the complete backward tracing technique.\n\n   **Quick version:**\n   - Where does bad value originate?\n   - What called this with bad value?\n   - Keep tracing up until you find the source\n   - Fix at source, not at symptom\n\n### Phase 2: Pattern Analysis\n\n**Find the pattern before fixing:**\n\n1. **Find Working Examples**\n   - Locate similar working code in same codebase\n   - What works that's similar to what's broken?\n\n2. **Compare Against References**\n   - If implementing pattern, read reference implementation COMPLETELY\n   - Don't skim - read every line\n   - Understand the pattern fully before applying\n\n3. **Identify Differences**\n   - What's different between working and broken?\n   - List every difference, however small\n   - Don't assume \"that can't matter\"\n\n4. **Understand Dependencies**\n   - What other components does this need?\n   - What settings, config, environment?\n   - What assumptions does it make?\n\n### Phase 3: Hypothesis and Testing\n\n**Scientific method:**\n\n1. **Form Single Hypothesis**\n   - State clearly: \"I think X is the root cause because Y\"\n   - Write it down\n   - Be specific, not vague\n\n2. **Test Minimally**\n   - Make the SMALLEST possible change to test hypothesis\n   - One variable at a time\n   - Don't fix multiple things at once\n\n3. **Verify Before Continuing**\n   - Did it work? Yes → Phase 4\n   - Didn't work? Form NEW hypothesis\n   - DON'T add more fixes on top\n\n4. **When You Don't Know**\n   - Say \"I don't understand X\"\n   - Don't pretend to know\n   - Ask for help\n   - Research more\n\n### Phase 4: Implementation\n\n**Fix the root cause, not the symptom:**\n\n1. **Create Failing Test Case**\n   - Simplest possible reproduction\n   - Automated test if possible\n   - One-off test script if no framework\n   - MUST have before fixing\n   - Use the `ultrapowers:test-driven-development` skill for writing proper failing tests\n\n2. **Implement Single Fix**\n   - Address the root cause identified\n   - ONE change at a time\n   - No \"while I'm here\" improvements\n   - No bundled refactoring\n\n3. **Verify Fix**\n   - Test passes now?\n   - No other tests broken?\n   - Issue actually resolved?\n   - If the bug was meaningful or systemic, use `ultrapowers:learning-from-bugs` before declaring the fix complete.\n\n4. **If Fix Doesn't Work**\n   - STOP\n   - Count: How many fixes have you tried?\n   - If < 3: Return to Phase 1, re-analyze with new information\n   - **If ≥ 3: STOP and question the architecture (step 5 below)**\n   - DON'T attempt Fix #4 without architectural discussion\n\n5. **If 3+ Fixes Failed: Question Architecture**\n\n   **Pattern indicating architectural problem:**\n   - Each fix reveals new shared state/coupling/problem in different place\n   - Fixes require \"massive refactoring\" to implement\n   - Each fix creates new symptoms elsewhere\n\n   **STOP and question fundamentals:**\n   - Is this pattern fundamentally sound?\n   - Are we \"sticking with it through sheer inertia\"?\n   - Should we refactor architecture vs. continue fixing symptoms?\n\n   **Discuss with your human partner before attempting more fixes**\n\n   This is NOT a failed hypothesis - this is a wrong architecture.\n\n## Red Flags - STOP and Follow Process\n\nIf you catch yourself thinking:\n- \"Quick fix for now, investigate later\"\n- \"Just try changing X and see if it works\"\n- \"Add multiple changes, run tests\"\n- \"Skip the test, I'll manually verify\"\n- \"It's probably X, let me fix that\"\n- \"I don't fully understand but this might work\"\n- \"Pattern says X but I'll adapt it differently\"\n- \"Here are the main problems: [lists fixes without investigation]\"\n- Proposing solutions before tracing data flow\n- **\"One more fix attempt\" (when already tried 2+)**\n- **Each fix reveals new problem in different place**\n\n**ALL of these mean: STOP. Return to Phase 1.**\n\n**If 3+ fixes failed:** Question the architecture (see Phase 4.5)\n\n## your human partner's Signals You're Doing It Wrong\n\n**Watch for these redirections:**\n- \"Is that not happening?\" - You assumed without verifying\n- \"Will it show us...?\" - You should have added evidence gathering\n- \"Stop guessing\" - You're proposing fixes without understanding\n- \"Ultrathink this\" - Question fundamentals, not just symptoms\n- \"We're stuck?\" (frustrated) - Your approach isn't working\n\n**When you see these:** STOP. Return to Phase 1.\n\n## Common Rationalizations\n\n| Excuse | Reality |\n|--------|---------|\n| \"Issue is simple, don't need process\" | Simple issues have root causes too. Process is fast for simple bugs. |\n| \"Emergency, no time for process\" | Systematic debugging is FASTER than guess-and-check thrashing. |\n| \"Just try this first, then investigate\" | First fix sets the pattern. Do it right from the start. |\n| \"I'll write test after confirming fix works\" | Untested fixes don't stick. Test first proves it. |\n| \"Multiple fixes at once saves time\" | Can't isolate what worked. Causes new bugs. |\n| \"Reference too long, I'll adapt the pattern\" | Partial understanding guarantees bugs. Read it completely. |\n| \"I see the problem, let me fix it\" | Seeing symptoms ≠ understanding root cause. |\n| \"One more fix attempt\" (after 2+ failures) | 3+ failures = architectural problem. Question pattern, don't fix again. |\n\n## Quick Reference\n\n| Phase | Key Activities | Success Criteria |\n|-------|---------------|------------------|\n| **1. Root Cause** | Read errors, reproduce, check changes, gather evidence | Understand WHAT and WHY |\n| **2. Pattern** | Find working examples, compare | Identify differences |\n| **3. Hypothesis** | Form theory, test minimally | Confirmed or new hypothesis |\n| **4. Implementation** | Create test, fix, verify | Bug resolved, tests pass |\n\n## When Process Reveals \"No Root Cause\"\n\nIf systematic investigation reveals issue is truly environmental, timing-dependent, or external:\n\n1. You've completed the process\n2. Document what you investigated\n3. Implement appropriate handling (retry, timeout, error message)\n4. Add monitoring/logging for future investigation\n\n**But:** 95% of \"no root cause\" cases are incomplete investigation.\n\n## Supporting Techniques\n\n"

## Codex progress · 2026-08-12T08:52:39.458Z

The computer’s startup job did run, but OmniHarness itself is still not listening on port 3050. The restart controller is alive, so the failure is later in the chain: launchd started the controller, but the controller did not successfully bring up the runner. I’m narrowing that down from the job configuration, process tree, and its own logs now.

service as a\n# macOS launchd job. By default this is a LaunchDaemon so it starts after reboot\n# even if the Mac does not auto-login. Pass OMNIHARNESS_RESTART_LAUNCHD_SCOPE=user\n# if you explicitly want the older login-session LaunchAgent behavior.\n#\n# The service listens on OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_PORT (default 3099) and lets\n# you remotely restart the app (typically exposed via a cloudflared tunnel).\n#\n# Usage:\n#   ./scripts/install-restarter.sh            # install + start\n#   ./scripts/install-restarter.sh uninstall  # stop + remove\n#\n# Honors OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_PORT to pin a non-default port.\n# Honors OMNIHARNESS_RESTART_LAUNCHD_SCOPE=user|system to override the launchd\n# scope. The default is system.\n# Honors OMNIHARNESS_RESTART_LAUNCHD_DIR to override the plist directory.\n\nset -euo pipefail\n\nLABEL=\"com.omniharness.restart-control\"\nPORT=\"${OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_PORT:-3099}\"\nLAUNCHD_SCOPE=\"${OMNIHARNESS_RESTART_LAUNCHD_SCOPE:-system}\"\nCURRENT_USER=\"${SUDO_USER:-$(id -un)}\"\nCURRENT_UID=\"$(id -u \"$CURRENT_USER\")\"\nTARGET_HOME=\"$(eval \"printf '%s' ~${CURRENT_USER}\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\nif [[ -z \"$TARGET_HOME\" || \"$TARGET_HOME\" == \"~\"* ]]; then\n  TARGET_HOME=\"$HOME\"\nfi\nUSER_PLIST_DIR=\"$TARGET_HOME/Library/LaunchAgents\"\nSYSTEM_PLIST_DIR=\"/Library/LaunchDaemons\"\n\ncase \"$LAUNCHD_SCOPE\" in\n  system)\n    PLIST_DIR=\"${OMNIHARNESS_RESTART_LAUNCHD_DIR:-$SYSTEM_PLIST_DIR}\"\n    LAUNCHD_DOMAIN=\"system\"\n    LEGACY_DOMAIN=\"gui/${CURRENT_UID}\"\n    LEGACY_PLIST=\"$USER_PLIST_DIR/${LABEL}.plist\"\n    ;;\n  user)\n    PLIST_DIR=\"${OMNIHARNESS_RESTART_LAUNCHD_DIR:-$USER_PLIST_DIR}\"\n    LAUNCHD_DOMAIN=\"gui/${CURRENT_UID}\"\n    LEGACY_DOMAIN=\"\"\n    LEGACY_PLIST=\"\"\n    ;;\n  *)\n    echo \"[install-restarter] ERROR: OMNIHARNESS_RESTART_LAUNCHD_SCOPE must be 'system' or 'user'.\" >&2\n    exit 1\n    ;;\nesac\n\nPLIST=\"${PLIST_DIR}/${LABEL}.plist\"\nNEEDS_SUDO=0\nif [[ \"$LAUNCHD_SCOPE\" == \"system\" && \"$PLIST_DIR\" == \"$SYSTEM_PLIST_DIR\" && \"${EUID:-$(id -u)}\" -ne 0 ]]; then\n  NEEDS_SUDO=1\nfi\n\nrun_as_root() {\n  if [[ \"$NEEDS_SUDO\" -eq 1 ]]; then\n    sudo \"$@\"\n  else\n    \"$@\"\n  fi\n}\n\nlaunchctl_cmd() {\n  if [[ \"$NEEDS_SUDO\" -eq 1 ]]; then\n    sudo launchctl \"$@\"\n  else\n    launchctl \"$@\"\n  fi\n}\n\n# Repo root = parent of this script's directory.\nSCRIPT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"${BASH_SOURCE[0]}\")\" && pwd)\"\nREPO_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$SCRIPT_DIR/..\" && pwd)\"\n\nunload() {\n  local target_domain=\"$1\"\n\n  if launchctl_cmd print \"${target_domain}/${LABEL}\" >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n    launchctl_cmd bootout \"${target_domain}/${LABEL}\" 2>/dev/null || true\n    # bootout is asynchronous — wait until the label is actually gone so a\n    # subsequent bootstrap doesn't race it (launchd error 5: I/O error).\n    for _ in $(seq 1 20); do\n      launchctl_cmd print \"${target_domain}/${LABEL}\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || break\n      sleep 0.25\n    done\n  fi\n}\n\nunload_legacy_agent() {\n  if [[ -z \"${LEGACY_DOMAIN:-}\" || -z \"${LEGACY_PLIST:-}\" ]]; then\n    return\n  fi\n\n  if launchctl_cmd print \"${LEGACY_DOMAIN}/${LABEL}\" >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n    launchctl_cmd bootout \"${LEGACY_DOMAIN}/${LABEL}\" 2>/dev/null || true\n    for _ in $(seq 1 20); do\n      launchctl_cmd print \"${LEGACY_DOMAIN}/${LABEL}\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || break\n      sleep 0.25\n    done\n  fi\n\n  rm -f \"$LEGACY_PLIST\"\n}\n\nif [[ \"${1:-}\" == \"uninstall\" || \"${1:-}\" == \"remove\" ]]; then\n  echo \"[install-restarter] Stopping and removing ${LABEL}…\"\n  unload \"$LAUNCHD_DOMAIN\"\n  if [[ \"$LAUNCHD_SCOPE\" == \"system\" ]]; then\n    unload_legacy_agent\n  fi\n  rm -f \"$PLIST\"\n  echo \"[install-restarter] Removed $PLIST\"\n  exit 0\nfi\n\n# Resolve toolchain absolute paths — launchd agents do not inherit your shell PATH.\nPNPM_BIN=\"$(command -v pnpm || true)\"\nNODE_BIN=\"$(command -v node || true)\"\nif [[ -z \"$PNPM_BIN\" ]]; then\n  echo \"[install-restarter] ERROR: pnpm not found on PATH.\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\nif [[ -z \"$NODE_BIN\" ]]; then\n  echo \"[install-restarter] ERROR: node not found on PATH.\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\nNODE_DIR=\"$(dirname \"$NODE_BIN\")\"\n\n# Build a PATH that includes the node (nvm) bin dir plus common locations.\nAGENT_PATH=\"${NODE_DIR}:/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin\"\nUSER_NAME_BLOCK=\"\"\nif [[ \"$LAUNCHD_SCOPE\" == \"system\" ]]; then\n  USER_NAME_BLOCK=\"    <key>UserName</key>\n    <string>${CURRENT_USER}</string>\"\nfi\n\nif [[ \"$LAUNCHD_SCOPE\" == \"system\" ]]; then\n  unload_legacy_agent\nfi\n\nrun_as_root mkdir -p \"$PLIST_DIR\"\nmkdir -p \"$REPO_ROOT/.omniharness\"\n\necho \"[install-restarter] Writing $PLIST (port ${PORT})…\"\ncat > \"$PLIST\" <<PLIST_EOF\n<?xml version=\"1.0\" encoding=\"UTF-8\"?>\n<!DOCTYPE plist PUBLIC \"-//Apple//DTD PLIST 1.0//EN\" \"http://www.apple.com/DTDs/PropertyList-1.0.dtd\">\n<plist version=\"1.0\">\n<dict>\n    <key>Label</key>\n    <string>${LABEL}</string>\n\n    <!-- Source .env first so the restart-control web UI accepts the OmniHarness\n         password (OMNIHARNESS_AUTH_PASSWORD) and the app it spawns inherits the\n         same env. remote-restart.ts itself does not load .env. -->\n    <key>ProgramArguments</key>\n    <array>\n        <string>/bin/bash</string>\n        <string>-c</string>\n        <string>cd '${REPO_ROOT}'; set -a; [ -f .env ] &amp;&amp; . ./.env; set +a; exec '${PNPM_BIN}' run restart:control</string>\n    </array>\n\n${USER_NAME_BLOCK}\n\n    <key>WorkingDirectory</key>\n    <string>${REPO_ROOT}</string>\n\n    <key>EnvironmentVariables</key>\n    <dict>\n        <key>PATH</key>\n        <string>${AGENT_PATH}</string>\n        <key>HOME</key>\n        <string>${TARGET_HOME}</string>\n        <key>OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_PORT</key>\n        <string>${PORT}</string>\n    </dict>\n\n    <key>RunAtLoad</key>\n    <true/>\n\n    <key>KeepAlive</key>\n    <true/>\n\n    <key>StandardOutPath</key>\n    <string>${REPO_ROOT}/.omniharness/restart-control.out.log</string>\n\n    <key>StandardErrorPath</key>\n    <string>${REPO_ROOT}/.omniharness/restart-control.err.log</string>\n</dict>\n</plist>\nPLIST_EOF\n\n# Reload cleanly (idempotent): bootout if already loaded, then bootstrap.\nunload \"$LAUNCHD_DOMAIN\"\necho \"[install-restarter] Loading agent…\"\nlaunchctl_cmd bootstrap \"$LAUNCHD_DOMAIN\" \"$PLIST\"\n\n# Brief health check.\nsleep 3\nif launchctl_cmd print \"${LAUNCHD_DOMAIN}/${LABEL}\" 2>/dev/null | grep -q \"state = running\"; then\n  echo \"[install-restarter] ✓ Running. restart-control listening on http://0.0.0.0:${PORT}\"\n  echo browser/PWA is served by any runner with `dist/interface`. The other hosts\nare remote clients and never embed or start a runner:\n\n```bash\npnpm electron:build\npnpm electron:package:local\npnpm vscode:build\npnpm vscode:package\npnpm mobile:sync\n```\n\nOperational and security details:\n\n- [Runner deployment, data roots, proxies, backup, and smoke checks](docs/deployment/runner-operations.md)\n- [Authentication, sessions, credential storage, identity, and TLS](docs/security/authentication-and-tls.md)\n- [Desktop/editor migration, mobile/PWA limits, and v1 non-goals](docs/platforms/client-migration-and-limits.md)\n\nRun the separate restart control app if you want a small remote escape hatch\nwith its own password-gated interface:\n\n```bash\npnpm restart:control\n```\n\nIt listens on port `3099`. Open `http://localhost:3099` to see status, recent\nlogs, and buttons to start OmniHarness in dev or production mode. The web login\nuses `OMNIHARNESS_AUTH_PASSWORD_HASH` or `OMNIHARNESS_AUTH_PASSWORD` when either\nis configured, then falls back to `OMNIHARNESS_REMOTE_RESTART_PASSWORD`, then to\nthe generated token file.\n\nTo keep the restart controller alive across reboots even when macOS does not\nauto-login, install the boot-time daemon:\n\n```bash\n./scripts/install-restarter.sh\n```\n\nIt installs as a `LaunchDaemon` by default. If you explicitly want the older\nlogin-session `LaunchAgent` behavior, set\n`OMNIHARNESS_RESTART_LAUNCHD_SCOPE=user` before running the installer.\n\nThe script API creates `.omniharness/remote-restart-token` on first run and\naccepts bearer auth:\n\n```bash\ncurl -X POST \"http://HOST:3099/restart?mode=dev\" \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $(cat .omniharness/remote-restart-token)\"\n```\n\nUse `mode=prod` to launch `./omniharness` instead of `pnpm run dev`.\n\nFor public access through Cloudflare Tunnel, expose the restart app as a second\nhostname only if you intentionally want that public-tunnel deployment:\n\n```yaml\ningress:\n  - hostname: horse-battery-staple.omniharness.dev\n    service: http://localhost:3050\n  - hostname: restart-horse-battery-staple.omniharness.dev\n    service: http://localhost:3099\n  - service: http_status:404\n```\n\n## Optional Setup\n\nCreate a local env file only if you want password auth, phone pairing, public-origin links, or API-key based agent/model access:\n\n```bash\ncp .env.example .env\n```\n\nLocal agent CLIs that are already logged in usually work without API keys. If you want the supervisor model or runtime workers to use provider keys from the environment, fill in the relevant variables in `.env`.\n\n## Password Auth\n\nOmniHarness requires a password before the web UI can create an authenticated session or pair a phone.\n\nFor the simplest local setup, run the launcher and follow the prompt:\n\n```bash\n./omniharness\n```\n# Runner deployment and operations\n\n## Process model\n\nOne OmniHarness runner is one logical deployment with two co-located\nprocesses:\n\n- the API server owns SQLite, auth, supervision, PTYs, settings, files, and the\n  HTTP/SSE contract;\n- the ACP bridge owns agent processes and listens on loopback by default.\n\nClients connect only to the API server. Electron, iOS, Android, VS Code, and\nbrowser/PWA clients never connect directly to the bridge.\n\n`pnpm runner` starts the API server and starts or adopts a loopback bridge. The\nAPI remains available in a degraded state while a failed bridge retries. All\nstart, adopt, retry, refusal, failure, and shutdown choices emit named events.\n\n## Build and start\n\nBuild the shared static interface and start a normal runner:\n\n```bash\npnpm install\npnpm build\npnpm runner\n```\n\nThe default API/UI address is `http://0.0.0.0:3050`; the managed bridge defaults\nto `http://127.0.0.1:7800`.\n\nPort `3050` is also the existing public-tunnel origin contract. Replacing the\nweb framework or static-interface implementation does not authorize changing\nthat deployed port or the configured public hostname. During a cutover, start\nthe replacement runner on the existing origin first and verify the public\nhealth endpoint before changing any tunnel route.\n\nSupported runner options:\n\n```text\n--host <address>\n--port <0-65535>\n--bridge-url <url>\n--static-dir <directory>\n--no-static\n```\n\nThe equivalent environment settings are:\n\n```text\nOMNIHARNESS_RUNNER_HOST\nOMNIHARNESS_RUNNER_PORT (PORT is a compatibility fallback)\nOMNIHARNESS_BRIDGE_URL\nOMNIHARNESS_STATIC_DIR\nOMNIHARNESS_NO_STATIC=1\nOMNIHARNESS_MANAGE_BRIDGE=0\n```\n\nWith the default build, the runner serves `dist/interface`. `--no-static` is a\nsupported API-only mode. If the default artifact is absent, startup continues\nand emits `runner.static_ui_missing`; if an explicitly supplied `--static-dir`\nis missing or invalid, startup fails. Never combine `--no-static` and\n`--static-dir`.\n\n## Instances, data, and locks\n\n`OMNIHARNESS_ROOT` selects the base data root. `OMNIHARNESS_INSTANCE=name`\nplaces one isolated deployment under\n`$OMNIHARNESS_ROOT/instances/name`. Each instance owns:\n\n```text\nsqlite.db\n.omniharness-auth.key\nrunner.lock.json\nbridge.lock.json\napp-data/run-data/\n```\n\nThe SQLite database and worker JSONL files are one consistency domain. Do not\npoint two live runners at the same instance root. A second process refuses an\nowned runner lock. Separate instances need separate API ports and bridge URLs.\n\n## Private access with Tailscale\n\nTailscale Serve is the preferred remote-access boundary for a Mac runner. From\nthe repository root, run:\n\n```bash\npnpm setup:tailscale\n```\n\nThe setup command validates the connected tailnet, configures persistent private\nHTTPS proxying to `http://127.0.0.1:3050`, and manages only these ignored `.env`\nsettings:\n\n```text\nOMNIHARNESS_PUBLIC_ORIGIN=https://device.tailnet.ts.net\nOMNIHARNESS_RUNNER_HOST=127.0.0.1\n```\n\nRestart `./omniharness`, check `tailscale serve status`, and open the printed URL\nfrom an allowed tailnet device. Use `pnpm setup:tailscale -- --dry-run` to inspect\nthe operation, `--force` to preserve and replace conflicting origin/host values,\nor `--reset` to disable the owned HTTPS listener and remove its managed settings.\n`--force` never replaces unrelated Tailscale Serve routes. The command never\nenables Funnel and never proxies port `7800`.\n\n## TLS and reverse proxies\n\nThe Node runner currently terminates HTTP. For any non-loopback deployment,\nterminate HTTPS at a reverse proxy or private tunnel and expose only the API/UI\nport. Keep the ACP bridge on loopback.\n\nForwarding headers are ignored unless the socket peer matches an explicit\ncomma-separated `OMNIHARNESS_TRUSTED_PROXIES` rule. Rules may be exact IPs or\nIPv4 CIDRs:\n\n```text\nOMNIHARNESS_TRUSTED_PROXIES=127.0.0.1,10.20.0.0/16\nOMNIHARNESS_PUBLIC_ORIGIN=https://runner.example.net\n```\n\nConfigure the proxy to replace, not append untrusted client values for,\n`Forwarded`/`X-Forwarded-*`. Never use a catch-all trusted-proxy range.\n\n## Backup and restore\n\nStop the runner before a filesystem backup. Copy the entire instance directory,\nincluding the database, auth key, worker streams, locks, settings, and managed\nmetadata. Exclude only transient build caches. Also back up the deployment's\npassword-hash configuration separately.\n\nRestore into an empty instance path while the runner is stopped, preserve file\npermissions, then start it with the same `OMNIHARNESS_ROOT` and\n`OMNIHARNESS_INSTANCE`. The auth key is required to read encrypted settings and\nvalidate existing sessions. If it is intentionally omitted, rotate the\npassword and expect every client to authenticate again.\n\nClient profile lists and platform-secure bearer sessions are client state, not\npart of a runner backup.\n\n## Disposable smoke check\n\nThis uses an isolated root and a hidden prompt. The password is not passed as a\ncommand-line argument or printed:\n\n```bash\nsmoke_root=\"$(mktemp -d \"${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/omniharness-smoke.XXXXXX\")\"\ntrap 'test -n \"$smoke_root\" && rm -rf -- \"$smoke_root\"' EXIT\nread -r -s -p \"Temporary runner password: \" smoke_password\nprintf '\\n'\nexport OMNIHARNESS_AUTH_PASSWORD=\"$smoke_password\"\nunset smoke_password\nOMNIHARNESS_ROOT=\"$smoke_root\" \\\nOMNIHARNESS_INSTANCE=smoke \\\npnpm runner --host 127.0.0.1 --port 3059 --no-static\n```\n\nIn another terminal, check liveness with:\n\n```bash\ncurl --fail --silent --show-error http://127.0.0.1:3059/api/healthz\n```\n\nPress Ctrl-C in the runner terminal. The trap removes only the temporary root\ncreated by this command.\n\n(exit 0)\n## lock files\n{\n  \"host\" => \"0.0.0.0\"\n  \"pid\" => 92692\n  \"port\" => 3050\n  \"startedAt\" => 1786505423751\n}\n{\n  \"bridgeUrl\" => \"http://127.0.0.1:7800\"\n  \"pid\" => 92692\n  \"startedAt\" => 1786505424486\n}\nrunner.lock.json modified=2026-08-12 05:30:23 +0200 owner=masterman:staff\nbridge.lock.json modified=2026-08-12 05:30:24 +0200 owner=masterman:staff\n\n(exit 0)\n## health\ncurl: (7) Failed to connect to 127.0.0.1 port 3050 after 0 ms: Couldn't connect to server\n\n(exit 7)"

const cmds = [
  ["status and listeners", "lsof -nP -iTCP:3099 -sTCP:LISTEN; curl -sS --max-time 3 http://127.0.0.1:3099/status", 10000],
  ["full controller tree", "ps -axo pid,ppid,user,lstart,state,command | awk '$1==291 || $2==291 || $1==3480 || $2==3480 || $1==3615 || $2==3615 || $1==3629 || $2==3629 || $1==3645 || $2==3645 {print}'", 10000],
  ["controller current boot stdout", "awk 'BEGIN{p=0} /Done in 2\\.4s using pnpm v11\\.2\\.2/{p=1} p{print}' .omniharness/restart-control.out.log", 10000],
  ["runner last 100", "tail -n 100 .omniharness/remote-restart.log", 30000],
  ["boot startup references", "rg -n -C 4 \"server\\.listen|controller\\.(start|restart)|restart\\(|start\\(|auto.?start|on boot|after reboot|RunAtLoad\" scripts/remote-restart.ts src/server/restart-control.ts README.md scripts tests docs --glob '!docs/codex-history/**' --glob '!docs/superpowers/plans/**' --glob '!docs/superpowers/specs/**' --glob '!docs/superpowers/learnings/**'", 30000],
  ["script tail", "sed -n '520,760p' scripts/remote-restart.ts && sed -n '1,280p' src/server/restart-control.ts && sed -n '280,560p' src/server/restart-control.ts", 40000],
  ["package lifecycle", "node -e 'const p=require(\"./package.json\"); for (const [k,v] of Object.entries(p.scripts||{})) if (/restart|start|runner/.test(k)) console.log(`${k}: ${v}`)'", 10000]
];
const out = await Promise.all(cmds.map(async ([label, cmd, max]) => {
  const r = await tools.exec_command({cmd, workdir:"/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness", yield_time_ms:10000, max_output_tokens:max});
  return `## ${label}\n${r.output}\n(exit ${r.exit_code})`;
}));

## Visible reasoning summary · 2026-08-12T08:53:25.595Z

**Investigating LaunchDaemon restart behavior**

<!-- /codex-event -->

<!-- codex-event:{"kind":"progress","timestamp":"2026-08-12T08:53:28.942Z","phase":"commentary"} -->
## Codex progress · 2026-08-12T08:53:28.942Z

The root cause is becoming clear: the installed launchd job is configured to start only the small restart-control service on port 3099. Its code never restores or starts the OmniHarness runner on boot, even though it still has a stale record saying production was the last mode. After the reboot, the old runner process disappeared, launchd correctly brought back only the controller, and nothing issued the separate “start production” action. I’m checking the history and tests to confirm whether this was an installation/configuration regression or an unimplemented promise before changing it.

<!-- /codex-event -->

learnings\ndocs/superpowers/learnings/2026-07-25-restart-daemons-need-stable-codex-tool-discovery.md\n# Restart Daemons Need Stable Codex Tool Discovery\n\n**Date:** 2026-07-25\n**Context:** OmniHarness development startup through the macOS restart controller and Cloudflare Tunnel\n**Symptom:** The restart controller accepted a development start, but the agent runtime timed out after 90 seconds, the web ports never opened, and the saved public hostname returned Cloudflare 502.\n**Root Cause:** The LaunchDaemon environment could not find the temporary Codex tool directory inherited by interactive shells. OmniHarness knew about the native binary in Codex.app but not the binary bundled with ChatGPT.app, so it could not create the required `apply_patch` and `applypatch` shims and rejected its own runtime as unready. Separately, `.env` still pointed at an old tunnel hostname rather than the hostname served by the installed connector.\n**Fix:** Added the ChatGPT.app native Codex binary to stable application discovery, kept the strict tool readiness check, and updated `OMNIHARNESS_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` to the active tunnel hostname.\n**Verification:** `pnpm exec vitest run tests/server/agent-runtime/tool-env.test.ts tests/server/dev/bridge-health.test.ts --reporter=dot` passed 8 tests. Starting development through `POST /restart?mode=dev` produced listeners on ports 7800, 3050, and 3035; the runtime doctor reported `tools.ok=true`; and the configured public origin returned HTTP 200.\n**Prevention:** Background startup must discover required binaries from stable install locations rather than temporary interactive-shell paths. When diagnosing a tunnel 502, verify both the local listeners and that the saved public hostname matches the active connector configuration.\n**Skill/Doc Updates:** No general skill update was needed because the debugging and completion skills already require tracing each component boundary and verifying the real public endpoint. This note records the OmniHarness-specific stable binary and hostname checks.\ndocs/superpowers/learnings/2026-08-02-restart-controller-does-not-supervise-runner.md\n# Restart Control Is Not Process Supervision\n\n**Date:** 2026-08-02\n**Context:** OmniHarness production runner, public tunnel, and remote restart controller\n**Symptom:** The public OmniHarness address returned HTTP 502 and the interface did not load, while the restart controller itself remained available.\n**Root Cause:** No process was listening on the managed runner or bridge ports. The runner had served requests normally and then disappeared without an application exception or controlled-shutdown entry. Its PID and runner lock records were stale. The restart controller reports and restarts processes on request, but it does not supervise a child after startup or automatically recover an unexpected exit. The external reason that terminated the child was not present in the application log or macOS crash reports.\n**Fix:** The existing production runner was restarted through the authorized restart controller, which cleared stale process ownership, started the runner and bridge again, and restored the public tunnel target.\n**Verification:** Local checks returned HTTP 200 from `/` and `/api/runtime/bootstrap`; the bridge health endpoint returned HTTP 200; the public address returned HTTP 200 from `/` and `/api/runtime/bootstrap`; named events showed `runner.started` followed by `runner.bridge_ready`; and the managed listener remained present on port 3050 during the final stability check.\n**Prevention:** Do not treat a live restart controller as proof that the managed runner is live. Outage checks must inspect the managed ports and both local and public endpoints. A future reliability change should make the controller record child exit status and either supervise production with bounded restart backoff or delegate production ownership to a service manager that does.\n**Skill/Doc Updates:** No general skill update was needed because the existing debugging and control-plane guidance already requires checking every component boundary and making lifecycle decisions observable. This note records the OmniHarness-specific gap for a future supervision change.\ndocs/superpowers/learnings/2026-05-20-selected-server-bootstrap-must-not-be-demoted-by-cache.md\n# Selected Server Bootstrap Must Not Be Demoted By Cache\n\n## Context\n\nThe home app can receive an authoritative server snapshot for the selected run during bootstrap, then run the scoped frontend-cache hydration effects for that same run. If the cache wins after bootstrap, the view model treats the selected conversation as a preview and keeps the main panel in a loading state.\n\n## Learning\n\nFrontend cache hydration is only a preview fallback. It must not replace or demote an already server-authoritative snapshot for the same selected run.\n\n## Guardrail\n\nWhen changing event stream hydration, test the bootstrap path with `initialSnapshotSource: \"server\"` and a populated same-run cache. `hydrateFromCacheScope(selectedRunId)` should return false and preserve `snapshotSource: \"server\"`.\ndocs/superpowers/learnings/2026-08-05-web-stack-migrations-must-preserve-launcher-bootstrap.md\n# Web Stack Migrations Must Preserve Launcher Bootstrap\n\n**Date:** 2026-08-05\n**Context:** OmniHarness macOS installation and the Next-to-Vite runner migration\n**Symptom:** The public launcher still inspected `.next` files even though the production interface had moved to `dist/interface`, and it created password settings in `.env` without loading that file into the new Node runner.\n**Root Cause:** The interface migration replaced the web server/build system but did not explicitly carry forward the launcher's build-freshness and environment-loading responsibilities. The old framework had provided part of that bootstrap behavior implicitly.\n**Fix:** The runner commands and launcher port check now load `.env` through Node before resolving configuration. A Vite-specific content fingerprint validates the production interface and writes its marker only after a complete build. The launcher records the repository lockfile hash together with the Node native-module ABI, OS, and CPU after a successful dependency install instead of relying on pnpm's internal lockfile layout.\n**Verification:** Focused red/green coverage lives in `tests/scripts/omniharness-launcher.test.ts`, `tests/scripts/interface-build-state.test.ts`, and `tests/scripts/runner-help.test.ts`. The macOS installation workflow additionally starts the real launcher from a fresh checkout and verifies API health, HTML, a referenced asset, and the build marker.\n**Prevention:** Treat environment loading, dependency freshness, authentication bootstrap, build output, readiness URLs, and process startup as one installer contract whenever the web stack changes. A framework migration is incomplete until the real clean-checkout launcher proves that contract.\n**Skill/Doc Updates:** No general skill update was needed because the existing planning and verification skills already require boundary tracing and real end-to-end proof. The project README, deployment guide, tests, and release workflow now make this repository-specific contract explicit.\ndocs/superpowers/learnings/2026-07-25-restart-daemons-need-stable-codex-tool-discovery.md\n# Restart Daemons Need Stable Codex Tool Discovery\n\n**Date:** 2026-07-25\n**Context:** OmniHarness development startup through the macOS restart controller and Cloudflare Tunnel\n**Symptom:** The restart controller accepted a development start, but the agent runtime timed out after 90 seconds, the web ports never opened, and the saved public hostname returned Cloudflare 502.\n**Root Cause:** The LaunchDaemon environment could not find the temporary Codex tool directory inherited by interactive shells. OmniHarness knew about the native binary in Codex.app but not the binary bundled with ChatGPT.app, so it could not create the required `apply_patch` and `applypatch` shims and rejected its own runtime as unready. Separately, `.env` still pointed at an old tunnel hostname rather than the hostname served by the installed connector.\n**Fix:** Added the ChatGPT.app native Codex binary to stable application discovery, kept the strict tool readiness check, and updated `OMNIHARNESS_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` to the active tunnel hostname.\n**Verification:** `pnpm exec vitest run tests/server/agent-runtime/tool-env.test.ts tests/server/dev/bridge-health.test.ts --reporter=dot` passed 8 tests. Starting development through `POST /restart?mode=dev` produced listeners on ports 7800, 3050, and 3035; the runtime doctor reported `tools.ok=true`; and the configured public origin returned HTTP 200.\n**Prevention:** Background startup must discover required binaries from stable install locations rather than temporary interactive-shell paths. When diagnosing a tunnel 502, verify both the local listeners and that the saved public hostname matches the active connector configuration.\n**Skill/Doc Updates:** No general skill update was needed because the debugging and completion skills already require tracing each component boundary and verifying the real public endpoint. This note records the OmniHarness-specific stable binary and hostname checks.\n\n(exit 0)\n## old installer versions\ncommit=c236459a53eadf6f49ba1dd855aea524a2f1f4bf\nzsh:1: bad substitution\ncommit=e53aeb2277b64219cbaf4aa94bd586c07c67aa9f\nzsh:1: bad substitution\n\n(exit 1)"

<!-- codex-event:{"kind":"progress","timestamp":"2026-08-12T08:53:55.417Z","phase":"commentary"} -->
## Codex progress · 2026-08-12T08:53:55.417Z

This was not a one-off launch failure; it is a real reliability gap already documented in the repository: the controller is intentionally only a remote button panel, not a supervisor, and the “future reliability change” was never implemented. The previous production-mode record survived the reboot, but no startup code consumes it.

I’m fixing that exact gap now: the launchd-installed controller will restore the last running mode after a reboot, while an intentional stop will remain stopped. Because this changes a server startup decision, I’m following the project’s test-first and lifecycle-observability rules before editing.

<!-- /codex-event -->

Watch it fail. Write minimal code to pass.\n\n**Core principle:** If you didn't watch the test fail, you don't know if it tests the right thing.\n\n**Violating the letter of the rules is violating the spirit of the rules.**\n\n## When to Use\n\n**Always:**\n- New features\n- Bug fixes\n- Refactoring\n- Behavior changes\n\n**Exceptions (ask your human partner):**\n- Throwaway prototypes\n- Generated code\n- Configuration files\n\n**Do directly with targeted verification:**\n- tiny color, spacing, copy, or label changes,\n- one-line prompt wording changes,\n- docs-only wording changes,\n- mechanical edits where the expected result is obvious from the diff.\n\nDo not perform a full red-green cycle for a tiny mechanical edit unless it changes behavior or there is a real regression risk. For example, changing a button from gray to blue usually needs the edit plus a targeted visual or code check, not a new failing test.\n\nThinking \"skip TDD just this once\"? Stop. That's rationalization.\n\n## UI-Specific Note\n\nWhen the task is UI work, choose the design system and starting block before writing tests around the intended behavior and rendering. In Ultrapowers, that means `shadcn/ui` plus the closest available ShadCN Block for each new screen, form, or major surface unless the user says otherwise. Inspect https://ui.shadcn.com/blocks first, install or copy the selected block, and then adapt it. Before adding any individual control, check whether `shadcn/ui` already provides a suitable control and use/customize that control first.\n\nFor important app workflows, also consider whether `agentic-user-journey-testing` should be proposed. Do not run that higher-cost black-box test mode without explicit user approval.\n\n## The Iron Law\n\n```\nNO PRODUCTION CODE WITHOUT A FAILING TEST FIRST\n```\n\nWrite code before the test? Delete it. Start over.\n\n**No exceptions:**\n- Don't keep it as \"reference\"\n- Don't \"adapt\" it while writing tests\n- Don't look at it\n- Delete means delete\n\nImplement fresh from tests. Period.\n\n## Red-Green-Refactor\n\n```dot\ndigraph tdd_cycle {\n    rankdir=LR;\n    red [label=\"RED\\nWrite failing test\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\"#ffcccc\"];\n    verify_red [label=\"Verify fails\\ncorrectly\", shape=diamond];\n    green [label=\"GREEN\\nMinimal code\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\"#ccffcc\"];\n    verify_green [label=\"Verify passes\\nAll green\", shape=diamond];\n    refactor [label=\"REFACTOR\\nClean up\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\"#ccccff\"];\n    next [label=\"Next\", shape=ellipse];\n\n    red -> verify_red;\n    verify_red -> green [label=\"yes\"];\n    verify_red -> red [label=\"wrong\\nfailure\"];\n    green -> verify_green;\n    verify_green -> refactor [label=\"yes\"];\n    verify_green -> green [label=\"no\"];\n    refactor -> verify_green [label=\"stay\\ngreen\"];\n    verify_green -> next;\n    next -> red;\n}\n```\n\n### RED - Write Failing Test\n\nWrite one minimal test showing what should happen.\n\n<Good>\n```typescript\ntest('retries failed operations 3 times', async () => {\n  let attempts = 0;\n  const operation = () => {\n    attempts++;\n    if (attempts < 3) throw new Error('fail');\n    return 'success';\n  };\n\n  const result = await retryOperation(operation);\n\n  expect(result).toBe('success');\n  expect(attempts).toBe(3);\n});\n```\nClear name, tests real behavior, one thing\n</Good>\n\n<Bad>\n```typescript\ntest('retry works', async () => {\n  const mock = jest.fn()\n    .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error())\n    .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error())\n    .mockResolvedValueOnce('success');\n  await retryOperation(mock);\n  expect(mock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(3);\n});\n```\nVague name, tests mock not code\n</Bad>\n\n**Requirements:**\n- One behavior\n- Clear name\n- Real code (no mocks unless unavoidable)\n- Real product behavior is the target. Mocked behavior can never be the final deliverable or proof that the feature works.\n\n### Verify RED - Watch It Fail\n\n**MANDATORY. Never skip.**\n\n```bash\nnpm test path/to/test.test.ts\n```\n\nConfirm:\n- Test fails (not errors)\n- Failure message is expected\n- Fails because feature missing (not typos)\n\n**Test passes?** You're testing existing behavior. Fix test.\n\n**Test errors?** Fix error, re-run until it fails correctly.\n\n### GREEN - Minimal Code\n\nWrite simplest code to pass the test.\n\n<Good>\n```typescript\nasync function retryOperation<T>(fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) {\n    try {\n      return await fn();\n    } catch (e) {\n      if (i === 2) throw e;\n    }\n  }\n  throw new Error('unreachable');\n}\n```\nJust enough to pass\n</Good>\n\n<Bad>\n```typescript\nasync function retryOperation<T>(\n  fn: () => Promise<T>,\n  options?: {\n    maxRetries?: number;\n    backoff?: 'linear' | 'exponential';\n    onRetry?: (attempt: number) => void;\n  }\n): Promise<T> {\n  // YAGNI\n}\n```\nOver-engineered\n</Bad>\n\nDon't add features, refactor other code, or \"improve\" beyond the test.\n\n### Verify GREEN - Watch It Pass\n\n**MANDATORY.**\n\n```bash\nnpm test path/to/test.test.ts\n```\n\nConfirm:\n- Test passes\n- Other tests still pass\n- Output pristine (no errors, warnings)\n\n**Test fails?** Fix code, not test.\n\n**Other tests fail?** Fix now.\n\n### REFACTOR - Clean Up\n\nAfter green only:\n- Remove duplication\n- Improve names\n- Extract helpers\n\nKeep tests green. Don't add behavior.\n\n### Repeat\n\nNext failing test for next feature.\n\n## Good Tests\n\n| Quality | Good | Bad |\n|---------|------|-----|\n| **Minimal** | One thing. \"and\" in name? Split it. | `test('validates email and domain and whitespace')` |\n| **Clear** | Name describes behavior | `test('test1')` |\n| **Shows intent** | Demonstrates desired API | Obscures what code should do |\n\n## Why Order Matters\n\n**\"I'll write tests after to verify it works\"**\n\nTests written after code pass immediately. Passing immediately proves nothing:\n- Might test wrong thing\n- Might test implementation, not behavior\n- Might miss edge cases you forgot\n- You never saw it catch the bug\n\nTest-first forces you to see the test fail, proving it actually tests something.\n\n**\"I already manually tested all the edge cases\"**\n\nManual testing is ad-hoc. You think you tested everything but:\n- No record of what you tested\n- Can't re-run when code changes\n- Easy to forget cases under pressure\n- \"It worked when I tried it\" ≠ comprehensive\n\nAutomated tests are systematic. They run the same way every time.\n\n**\"Deleting X hours of work is wasteful\"**\n\nSunk cost fallacy. The time is already gone. Your choice now:\n- Delete and rewrite with TDD (X more hours, high confidence)\n- Keep it and add tests after (30 min, low confidence, likely bugs)\n\nThe \"waste\" is keeping code you can't trust. Working code without real tests is technical debt.\n\n**\"TDD is dogmatic, being pragmatic means adapting\"**\n\nTDD IS pragmatic:\n- Finds bugs before commit (faster than debugging after)\n- Prevents regressions (tests catch breaks immediately)\n- Documents behavior (tests show how to use code)\n- Enables refactoring (change freely, tests catch breaks)\n\n\"Pragmatic\" shortcuts = debugging in production = slower.\n\n**\"Tests after achieve the same goals - it's spirit not ritual\"**\n\nNo. Tests-after answer \"What does this do?\" Tests-first answer \"What should this do?\"\n\nTests-after are biased by your implementation. You test what you built, not what's required. You verify remembered edge cases, not discovered ones.\n\nTests-first force edge case discovery before implementing. Tests-after verify you remembered everything (you didn't).\n\n30 minutes of tests after ≠ TDD. You get coverage, lose proof tests work.\n\n## Common Rationalizations\n\n| Excuse | Reality |\n|--------|---------|\n| \"Too simple to test\" | Simple code breaks. Test takes 30 seconds. |\n| \"I'll test after\" | Tests passing immediately prove nothing. |\n| \"Tests after achieve same goals\" | Tests-after = \"what does this do?\" Tests-first = \"what should this do?\" |\n| \"Already manually tested\" | Ad-hoc ≠ systematic. No record, can't re-run. |\n| \"Deleting X hours is wasteful\" | Sunk cost fallacy. Keeping unverified code is technical debt. |\n| \"Keep as reference, write tests first\" | You'll adapt it. That's testing after. Delete means delete. |\n| \"Need to explore first\" | Fine. Throw away exploration, start with TDD. |\n| \"Test hard = design unclear\" | Listen to test. Hard to test = hard to use. |\n| \"TDD will slow me down\" | TDD faster than debugging. Pragmatic = test-first. |\n| \"Manual test faster\" | Manual doesn't prove edge cases. You'll re-test every change. |\n| \"Existing code has no tests\" | You're improving it. Add tests for existing code. |\n\n## Red Flags - STOP and Start Over\n\n- Code before test\n- Test after implementation\n- Test passes immediately\n- Can't explain why test failed\n- Tests added \"later\"\n- Rationalizing \"just this once\"\n- \"I already manually tested it\"\n- \"Tests after achieve the same purpose\"\n- \"It's about spirit not ritual\"\n- \"Keep as reference\" or \"adapt existing code\"\n- \"Already spent X hours, deleting is wasteful\"\n- \"TDD is dogmatic, I'm being pragmatic\"\n- \"This is different because...\"\n\n**All of these mean: Delete code. Start over with TDD.**\n\n## Example: Bug Fix\n\n**Bug:** Empty email accepted\n\n**RED**\n```typescript\ntest('rejects empty email', async () => {\n  const result = await submitForm({ email: '' });\n  expect(result.error).toBe('Email required');\n});\n```\n\n**Verify RED**\n```bash\n$ npm test\nFAIL: expected 'Email required', got undefined\n---\nname: instrumenting-control-planes\ndescription: Use when building, planning, reviewing, or debugging apps with backend decisions, agent workflows, long-running jobs, SSE/WebSocket streams, recovery paths, deletes, retries, or user-visible failures\n---\n\n# Instrumenting Control Planes\n\nBuild the control plane as a product surface. If the server decides, refuses, retries, spawns, deletes, recovers, gives up, or fails, that decision must be observable by users, operators, and tests.\n\nIf a React UI consumes the control plane through server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, or persisted runtime state, also use `client-server-state-invariants`.\n\n## Core Rule\n\nEvery meaningful server-side state transition emits a named, durable event. The UI and tests consume the same production event surface.\n\nDo not rely on snapshot diffs, rendered DOM state, logs only, or client-side re-derivation to prove what the server decided.\n\n## Hot Path Rule\n\nNo user bootstrap, catalog, status, reconnect, or recovery request may depend on a lock-prone database write path or external health probe finishing. Serve these paths from a bounded read model, memory cache, append-only event log, or stale-but-marked snapshot; queue reconciliation writes outside the request. Every dependency on a database or bridge call in a hot path needs a short timeout, cancellation, and an explicit degraded response.\n\nIf SQLite, local files, or a single-writer store is used, assume writer contention can freeze readers at the worst moment. Use one writer, WAL/busy-timeout discipline, short transactions, bounded queues, and headless chaos tests that run bootstrap/reload while writes are active. A 25s UI request is a control-plane failure, even if the database eventually returns.\n\n## Event Contract\n\nFor each backend or agent workflow, define:\n\n- named events for decisions: `worker.spawned`, `worker.reattached`, `plan.ready`, `plan.review.blocked`, `recovery.gave_up`, `conversation.delete_failed`;\n- payloads with stable ids, `prev`/`next` status when relevant, reason codes, and enough context to debug;\n- `error.surfaced` or an equivalent user-facing failure event for every failure the user should know about;\n- durable persistence or a bounded replay buffer so short disconnects do not erase history;\n- a scriptable read path: CLI, HTTP endpoint, sqlite query, or event log command.\n\nIf a decision cannot be asserted from this surface, the design is incomplete.\n\nIf debugging a control-plane failure reveals a reusable lesson, use `ultrapowers:learning-from-bugs` after the fix is verified.\n\n## Streaming Requirements\n\nFor SSE or WebSocket-like streams:\n\n- emit named events alongside any snapshot/update frame;\n- include monotonic event ids;\n- support resume from the last seen id;\n- replay missed events from a bounded buffer;\n- emit an explicit resync-required event when replay is impossible;\n- bootstrap from a snapshot, then tail events.\n\nSnapshots show current truth. Named events show why it became true.\n\n## Testing Standard\n\nControl-plane tests should usually be headless HTTP/SSE scenario tests, not browser tests. Browser automation is for UI behavior; control-plane tests assert server decisions directly.\n\nWrite scenario assertions as event transcripts:\n\n```ts\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.spawned\", { runId });\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.status\", { next: \"running\" });\nchaos.killServer();\nchaos.restartServer();\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.reattached\", { runId });\nexpect(events).not.toHaveEmitted(\"worker.recreated\", { runId });\n```\n\nChaos belongs outside production code paths: drop connections in the client, fail fetches in the harness, restart the server process, and use seeds for reproducibility.\n\n## Refuse These Shapes\n\n- silent early returns in server methods;\n- swallowed `catch` blocks;\n- client-side derivation of server decisions;\n- response-code-only user failures;\n- event streams without ids or resume;\n- tests that infer control-plane behavior from rendered state;\n- delete handlers that do not enumerate dependent rows or emit typed failure events;\n- recovery loops without terminal events such as `gave_up` or `abandoned`;\n- request handlers that wait on inline reconciliation writes, SQLite contention, or bridge `/doctor` checks before returning bootstrap data;\n- test-only behavior branches in production code.\n\n## Design Checklist\n\nBefore implementing a backend or agent workflow, answer:\n\n1. What decisions can this workflow make?\n2. What named event is emitted for each decision, including refusal and failure?\n3. Where are events persisted or buffered?\n4. How does a client resume after disconnect?\n5. Which request paths must keep working while persistence or bridge dependencies are slow, locked, or stale?\n6. How can a CLI or test harness inspect the exact event sequence without the UI?\n7. Which headless scenario proves the happy path, failure path, recovery path, terminal state, and hot-path timeout/degraded path?\n8. Which client state consumes these events, and how does it prove ownership, freshness, completeness, ordering, and replay/resync?\n\nIf the answer is \"the UI can figure it out\", name the missing event instead.\n---\nname: learning-from-bugs\ndescription: Use when a meaningful bug, regression, architecture flaw, storage issue, memory problem, persistence failure, control-plane failure, or system-level failure has been diagnosed or fixed\n---\n\n# Learning From Bugs\n\nWhen a bug teaches something reusable, capture the lesson before moving on.\n\n## Use This For\n\nUse this after diagnosing or fixing bugs that involve:\n\n- architecture or ownership boundaries,\n- storage, persistence, migrations, caching, or file paths,\n- memory management, leaks, lifecycle, cleanup, or resource pressure,\n- concurrency, queues, retries, resumability, or recovery,\n- control planes, event streams, background workers, or long-running jobs,\n- security, data loss, user trust, or incident-like failures,\n- any bug that took repeated attempts or revealed a wrong mental model.\n\nSkip it for typos, obvious one-line UI mistakes, throwaway test fixture fixes, or purely local churn that will not help future work.\n\n## Required Output\n\nWrite a short learning note to:\n\n`docs/superpowers/learnings/YYYY-MM-DD-<bug-or-lesson-slug>.md`\n\nCreate the directory if needed.\n\nUse this shape:\n\n```markdown\n# [Lesson Title]\n\n**Date:** YYYY-MM-DD\n**Context:** [project, feature, or subsystem]\n**Symptom:** [what failed]\n**Root Cause:** [the real cause, not the symptom]\n**Fix:** [what changed]\n**Verification:** [commands, tests, or checks that proved it]\n**Prevention:** [what future agents should do differently]\n**Skill/Doc Updates:** [nearby skills or docs updated, or why no general update was needed]\n```\n\nKeep it concrete. Future agents should be able to read the note and avoid the same mistake.\n\n## Update The System When The Lesson Generalizes\n\nIf the bug reveals a reusable rule, update the nearest skill or defaults doc in the same change. Examples:\n\n- debugging lesson -> `systematic-debugging`\n- verification gap -> `verification-before-completion`\n- control-plane or eventing lesson -> `instrumenting-control-planes`\n- React lifecycle or routing lesson -> `building-react-apps`\n- planning omission -> `writing-plans` or `brainstorming`\n\nDo not bury a general lesson only in a project note. The learning note records the case; the skill update changes future behavior.\n\n## Completion Gate\n\nBefore saying a meaningful bug is fixed, check:\n\n1. Is this lesson reusable?\n2. Is there a learning note?\n3. Did any skill or defaults doc need an update?\n4. Did verification prove the fix?\n\nIf the answer to 1 is yes, do not skip the note. If the answer to 3 is no, say why in the note.\n# Lifecycle Observability & Resilience Testing\n\n> What we keep getting wrong, why our tests don't catch it, and the discipline that fixes it.\n\nRelated: `docs/architecture/hot-path-responsiveness-and-resource-leaks.md`\ndocuments the May 2026 reload/worker-streaming latency incident and the\nresource-ownership rules for keeping page bootstrap, SSE, and worker stream\npaths responsive while agents are running.\n\n## Multi-runner client lifecycle\n\nEach saved runner profile owns one `RunnerConnection` state machine and at most\none main event stream. A registry starts every profile connection, not only the\nvisible one. Switching the visible workspace changes active managers/query\ncaches and terminal ownership without closing inactive main streams.\n\nEvery bootstrap, snapshot, frame, retry, timer, and mutation checks profile and\nlearned runner identity before applying state. Stored cursors include the\nrunner's stream epoch. `runner.stopping`, `auth.session_revoked`, and\n`stream.resync_required` are typed terminal/control outcomes, not generic\nnetwork failures.\n\nElectron, VS Code, and Capacitor host bridges have bounded stream delivery and\nmust cancel upstream work on overflow. Mobile suspension persists cursors,\ncloses streams, and reconnects on resume; it must not claim background\ndelivery. Platform lifecycle contract tests live under `tests/electron`,\n`tests/vscode`, `tests/mobile`, and `tests/runtime-api`; real two-runner and\neight-runner journeys live under `tests/e2e`.\n\n## Interactive protocol invariant\n\nAn advertised interactive agent capability is operational only when the complete user journey is implemented: receive the request, append it to the unified worker stream, render an actionable control in the main conversation, submit the exact protocol response, and append a terminal outcome before resolving the agent request. Side-panel-only controls do not satisfy this invariant. Every form shape and permission option emitted by a supported adapter must be covered by a finite wire-to-UI regression matrix.\n\nActionability comes only from the live runtime's `pendingElicitations` and\n`pendingPermissions`; an open worker-stream row proves only that a request\nexisted. After reattach or recreation, compare the durable open rows with the\nruntime-owned request ids and append terminal `cancelled` rows for any orphaned\nrequests before publishing the recovered worker. The UI may render the stream\nrows as history, but must never turn them back into response controls.\n\nRelated: `docs/architecture/timing-determinism-audit.md` records the May 20,\n2026 audit of race-prone frontend and control-plane patterns: cached snapshots\npretending to be authoritative, stale async callbacks, timer-owned actions,\n…3144 tokens truncated…chaos.killServer();\nchaos.restartServer();\nexpect(events).toHaveEmitted(\"worker.reattached\", { runId });\n// not \"worker.recreated\", not absence — explicitly reattached\n```\n\n### Rule 7: cleanup waits for server-owned background work\n\nLifecycle scenarios may call routes that intentionally return before a direct\nworker turn finishes. That is fine for product latency, but it is not fine for\ntest cleanup to delete `OMNIHARNESS_ROOT` while those server-owned tasks are\nstill writing worker JSONL, recovery rows, or named events.\n\nRequired shape:\n\n- Fire-and-forget direct/planning turns are registered as background\n  conversation tasks.\n- The lifecycle harness waits for registered background tasks before deleting\n  its temp root.\n- A timeout while waiting is a test failure, not harmless stderr after the test\n  already passed.\n- Do not paper over storage `ENOENT` from cleanup races by retrying file writes;\n  the ownership bug is untracked background work.\n\nProduction deletion follows the same ownership rule. A conversation delete\ninstalls a run-scoped deletion fence before cancelling workers or removing\nrows. Initial worker startup checks that fence both before and after spawn, so\na worker that finishes spawning during deletion is cancelled before its prompt\ncan run. The fence remains until every tracked background task for the run has\nsettled; a one-shot best-effort cancel is not sufficient.\n\nAsserting on rendered DOM state is the wrong granularity. We assert on the\nserver's *decisions*, which are now first-class events.\n\n### Rule 7: the test harness runs in isolation\n\nA separate Node process. A temporary `OMNI_HOME`. A fresh sqlite. A dedicated\nport. No collision with the real dev server. Tear it all down on exit.\nScenarios can run in parallel because each one owns its own server.\n\nThis isolation must not trust inherited runner environment. Agents spawned by\nOmniHarness may inherit server control variables, so Vitest always replaces\n`OMNIHARNESS_ROOT` with a newly created temporary root. Database startup in a\nrecognized test process refuses any non-temporary root, and ACP agent children\nmust not receive `OMNIHARNESS_ROOT`, `OMNIHARNESS_INSTANCE`, bridge addresses,\nor runner addresses. A test failure is always preferable to opening live data.\n\n### Rule 8: snapshot bootstrap, event tail thereafter\n\nEvery harness client starts by calling `GET /api/events?snapshot=1` for state\ntruth, then opens the SSE stream with `Last-Event-ID` set to the id at\nsnapshot time. This is the canonical pattern. The UI should do this too;\nright now it doesn't, which is a latent bug.\n\n### Rule 9: snapshots declare scoped completeness\n\nAn event snapshot may be a full replacement for a selected run's transcript,\nor it may be only a partial update. The payload must say which one it is.\n\n`messageScope` carries the contract:\n\n- `runIds`: the conversation run ids whose message set the payload is talking\n  about. For promoted implementation runs this includes the parent planning\n  run plus the selected implementation run.\n- `complete`: `true` means the payload contains the complete message set for\n  those run ids, so absent messages may be removed locally. `false` or a\n  missing scope means the payload is additive and must not erase durable\n  messages already known to the client.\n\nNever infer completeness from \"this snapshot contains a message for that run.\"\nThat was the race behind the disappearing preflight confirmation: a partial\nlive update containing only the user checkpoint looked authoritative enough to\nerase the supervisor confirmation from frontend state.\n\n`snapshotScope` extends the same idea to bounded streams that live in\nartifact storage (execution events, supervisor interventions, planning review\nfindings). Each entry reports `{limit, complete, oldestCreatedAt}`. Clients\nthat want older records keep a cursor (`oldestCreatedAt`) and request a\ndeeper window; clients that just need the head trust the snapshot and stop.\n\n### Append-only artifact streams\n\nThe bulky payload fields of these record families now live in JSONL files\nunder `<projectPath>/.omniharness/run-data/<runId>/`, not in SQLite:\n\n- `execution_events.details` → `execution-events.jsonl`\n- `supervisor_interventions.{prompt,summary}` → `supervisor-interventions.jsonl`\n- `planning_review_findings.{details,recommendation}` → `planning-review-findings.jsonl`\n- `worker_entries` (per-worker) → `workers/<workerId>.jsonl`\n\nThe metadata layer (`artifact_streams`) keeps a row per (runId, kind, ownerId)\nwith `latest_seq`, `latest_record_id`, `compacted_at`, and the resolved\n`project_path` + `relative_root_path`. Each domain row keeps a content hash\nand a short preview so list views don't need to read the artifact. Failure\nmodes surface through named events:\n\n- `artifact.append_failed` — JSONL write or lock acquisition failed.\n- `artifact.metadata_mismatch` — `latest_seq` and the file tail disagree.\n- `artifact.compaction_failed` / `artifact.compaction_completed` — terminal-run\n  sweep result.\n- `artifact.payload_missing` — domain row points at an artifact line that\n  isn't on disk (run `pnpm exec tsx scripts/artifact-repair.ts`).\n- `artifact.backfill_failed` — emitted by the migration script.\n\nSnapshot readers tolerate hydration failures: rows render from the preview\ncolumn and emit `artifact.payload_missing` so an operator can repair.\n\n### Rule 10: `awaiting_user` implies a visible question\n\nA selected run with `status=\"awaiting_user\"` must include a visible supervisor\nmessage with kind `clarification` or `implementation_confirmation`. The\ntransition that creates this state must persist the clarification row, the\nsupervisor message, the run status, and the lifecycle event before notifying\nSSE clients.\n\nIf a snapshot violates this invariant, it surfaces a `Lifecycle` frontend\nerror instead of leaving the UI stuck on \"Loading Omni's question\". The error\nis not a substitute for the invariant; it is the safety rail that makes any\nfuture violation observable.\n\n### Rule 11: terminal user actions retire recovery ownership first\n\nA user stop is not complete when only `runs.status` and `workers.status` are\nterminal. Before publishing the terminal run state, the server synchronously\ncancels volatile and durable wakes, clears wake leases, and resolves every open\nrecovery incident with its normal named event. Otherwise a later sync or timer\nstill owns permission to reactivate the run. Once cancelled, the run rejects\nlate live-worker snapshots from the interrupted turn even when the provider\ncontinues reporting `working` briefly.\n\nConversation deletion follows the same ownership rule. Before deleting a\nworker or run, enumerate and delete every row that references it, including\ncredential allocations and token usage. Route tests must insert a row in each\nworker-referencing table so new foreign keys cannot silently make deletion\npartial or impossible.\n\n### Rule 12: dependent selections cross the boundary together\n\nWorker type, account, model, and effort are one compound composer selection.\nThe value shown in the composer, sent to prewarm, and submitted to conversation\ncreation must come from the same compatibility resolution. A raw account id\nfrom a previously selected worker type is not an explicit preference for the\nnew worker.\n\nRun selection restores the whole compound selection, including the account.\nOptimistic run snapshots must carry the same selection fields as the durable\nrun so selecting a newly created conversation cannot replace an explicit\naccount with `auto` before the authoritative snapshot arrives.\n\nThe server still validates every explicit account selection, but it does so\nbefore creating plan, run, worker, message, or artifact state. Rejections use\nthe original client error status and emit `error.surfaced` with a stable code;\nthey never leave a partially created conversation for snapshots to discover.\n\n---\n\n## Anti-patterns to refuse on sight\n\nThese are the specific shapes of code that produce the bugs in this document.\nReviewers should reject these on sight.\n\n1. **Silent early returns in server methods.**\n   ```ts\n   if (hasLeftoverState) return; // ← BUG\n   ```\n   If a method refuses to proceed, it emits an event explaining why.\n\n2. **try/catch that swallows.**\n   ```ts\n   try { await doThing(); } catch { /* ignore */ } // ← BUG\n   ```\n   Either rethrow, or emit `error.surfaced` with the context. Never both\n   silent and continuing.\n\n3. **Client-side derivation of server decisions.**\n   If the server decided something (plan is ready, recovery gave up), the\n   server emits the event. The client does not re-derive it. If the client\n   is computing `derivePlanningStatus()` from raw fields, that is a missing\n   server event.\n\n4. **SSE frames without `id:`.**\n   Every frame has an id. No exceptions.\n\n5. **State diffs as the primary observability mechanism.**\n   \"The UI will figure out what happened by diffing snapshots\" is not an\n   observability strategy. Name the event.\n\n6. **Fault injection inside production code paths.**\n   `if (process.env.CHAOS) throw new Error(\"boom\")` does not appear in\n   server code. Chaos is a client-side concern.\n\n7. **Tests that boot Chromium to assert on control-plane behaviour.**\n   Use the HTTP/SSE harness. Reserve Playwright for actual UI behaviour\n   (rendering, input handling, layout).\n\n8. **DELETE handlers that don't enumerate dependent rows.**\n   If a row has foreign-key dependents, the DELETE handler either deletes\n   them in a transaction or emits `conversation.delete_failed` with the\n   blocking table/row. Never let the FK error reach the client as a raw 500.\n\n9. **Recovery loops without a terminal state.**\n   If `autoAttemptCount` keeps incrementing without an `abandoned` or\n   `gave_up` terminal event, there is no way for anyone — user, UI, test —\n   to know the system has given up. Always have a terminal state, and\n   always emit it.\n\n10. **Test-only code paths.**\n    `if (process.env.NODE_ENV === \"test\")` in production code is a bug. The\n    test harness should be able to drive production code paths from the\n    outside. The only exception is the dev-only `/api/events/log` polling\n    endpoint, which is build-stripped, has no behavioural effect, and exists\n    purely as a read-side affordance.\n\n---\n\n## How to add a new server-side decision\n\nThe checklist, every time:\n\n1. Pick the event name. Format: `domain.verb` (`worker.spawned`,\n   `plan.review.blocked`). Past tense for things that happened, no\n   \"will/should\".\n2. Define the payload shape in `src/server/events/named-events.ts` (TypeScript\n   union, exported).\n3. At the point of decision, call `emitNamedEvent(\"domain.verb\", payload)`.\n   The emitter writes to the ring buffer and triggers the SSE notifier.\n4. If the decision is a *failure* the user should see, also emit\n   `error.surfaced` with a stable code and human message.\n5. Write a scenario test that asserts the event fires under the expected\n   conditions. If the test cannot assert this, the event is not specific\n   enough — go back to step 1.\n6. The UI may or may not consume the event. That is a separate choice. The\n   event exists regardless.\n\nIf you find yourself unable to complete step 5, the design is wrong, not the\ntest.\n\n---\n\n## How to debug \"the user reports X did not happen\"\n\nThe procedure:\n\n1. Open the event log for the run: `GET /api/events/log?runId=<id>`.\n2. Look for the named event corresponding to X.\n3. If it is present: the server did the thing. The bug is in the UI or in\n   the user's mental model.\n4. If it is absent: the server did not do the thing. Find the code path that\n   *would* have emitted it, and find the early-return / swallowed error /\n   silent branch that prevented it. Fix that, and emit the appropriate event\n   (success or failure).\n5. If the code path doesn't emit anything in either branch: that is the bug.\n   Add the event per \"How to add a new server-side decision.\"\n\nEvery reported bug should land at one of these four answers. If the answer is\n\"I don't know, the server is a black box\", we have failed Rule 1.\n\n---\n\n## Worker conversation content vs. lifecycle events\n\nThese two surfaces are distinct and must stay distinct.\n\n- **Lifecycle events** (`worker.spawned`, `worker.status`,\n  `worker.terminal`, `recovery.*`, etc.) live in the ring buffer and on\n  the SSE stream. They are typed in `src/server/events/named-events.ts`\n  and emitted via `emitNamedEvent`. Their purpose is observability:\n  what decision did the server make.\n- **Worker conversation content** (what the worker said, what tools\n  it called, what the user typed, what the supervisor steered) lives\n  in the unified worker stream — one append-only JSONL per worker. The\n  canonical content cursor is `entry.seq`, not the global SSE id. SSE\n  emits a `worker.entry_appended` named event carrying only `{\n  workerId, seq }`; the entry body is fetched via\n  `GET /api/workers/:workerId/entries?afterSeq=`. See\n  `docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md`.\n\nTwo cursors deliberately: the global SSE id covers liveness/resync;\n`entry.seq` covers content. SSE frames are wake-up hints, never the\nauthority on content.\n\nComplete-state notifications need notification identity, not payload identity.\nContent-addressing a replacement entry silently collapses a valid `A → B → A`\nsequence into `A → B`, leaving replay on the wrong state. Assign a fresh entry\nidentity when each notification is admitted, and reserve idempotent deduplication\nfor retries that carry the same already-assigned entry id. Regression coverage\nfor complete-list projections must include both payload recurrence and authority\nrecurrence (for example, session `A → B → A`).\n\n## Open follow-ups\n\n- Backfill named events at every existing decision site (see implementation\n  plan).\n- Adopt SSE-spec compliance in the UI client (snapshot bootstrap +\n  `Last-Event-ID` resume). Today the UI just refetches a snapshot, which\n  works but is wasteful.\n- Audit every server `try/catch` and every early-`return` for missing\n  events. This is a one-time sweep; new code is held to the rules above\n  going forward.\n- Build out the headless chaos harness and the scenario catalog.\n\n---\n\n## Audit log\n\n### `try/catch` sweep — first pass\n\nWalked every `} catch {` in `src/server/**` and `src/app/api/**`. Findings:\n\n- **Legitimate silent fallbacks** (kept as-is, comment explains why):\n  `restart-control.ts` process-liveness probes (`process.kill(pid, 0)`),\n  `restart-control.ts:264` lsof-empty no-listener case,\n  `conversations/create.ts:257` opportunistic snapshot persist (the\n  function's user-visible state still comes from the `askAgent`\n  response, not the dropped snapshot),\n  `planning/review.ts:313` reviewer snapshot persist (same shape),\n  parse-error fallbacks in `plans/readiness-pipeline.ts`,\n  `planning/artifacts.ts`, `auth/guards.ts`, `projects/canonicalize.ts`.\n  Each catches an internal probe that cannot affect user-visible state.\n  These match the rare-exception carve-out for idempotency / probe\n  catches.\n\n- **Bugs that were already fixed in phase 2** (no remaining silent\n  failure in this category):\n  - Plan-review leftover-state silent throw → now emits\n    `plan.review.blocked` + `error.surfaced` (`planning/review.ts`).\n  - Recovery exhaustion silent cap → now emits `recovery.gave_up` +\n    `error.surfaced` (`runs/recovery-incidents.ts`).\n  - Delete conversation FK 500 → now returns 409 with\n    `conversation.delete_failed` + `error.surfaced` (`api/runs/[id]`).\n\n- **No new offenders found in the first pass.** New code is held to the\n  rule via the AGENTS.md entry; reviewer should reject silent catches on\n  state-mutating paths.\n\nFuture audit passes should look at:\n- ~~Early-return sites in `supervisor/observer.ts` (decision branches).~~\n  **Done.** All 16 `stopRunObserver` call sites now pass a typed\n  `reason` (`run_terminated`, `run_failed`, `cwd_mismatch`,\n  `snapshot_invalid`, `quota_exhausted`, `fatal_bridge_error`,\n  `explicit`). `supervisor.stopped` is emitted from inside\n  `stopRunObserver` itself, guarded by `wasActive` so bare stop calls\n  on never-started runs don't spam the stream. Scenario:\n  `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/supervisor-stopped.test.ts`.\n- ~~Every `if (!latestRun)` short-circuit that stops the observer.~~\n  Covered by the above — those now emit `supervisor.stopped` with\n  reason `run_terminated`.\n\n### Claude model gateway lifecycle\n\nThe managed Claude model gateway follows the same control-plane rules as workers and\nsessions. One server singleton owns its redacted status, monotonic revision, current\noperation, OAuth polling, and model cache. The operational API and canonical event\nsnapshot expose that same status; clients must not infer readiness from cached models.\n\nInstall, start, stop, OAuth, and model-refresh decisions emit typed\n`claude_gateway.*` events on the existing ring buffer. User-relevant failures also\nemit `error.surfaced` with a stable `claude_gateway.*` code (or the existing exact\n`process.*` code). Tokens, provider auth-file contents, and OAuth URLs never enter\nevents or snapshots.\n\nAt boot, enabled configurations are checked after database readiness without blocking\non downloads or OAuth. Managed mode adopts or starts only a process whose saved PID\nand command identity prove OmniHarness ownership. External mode performs bounded\nreadiness requests and never signals a process. Stop disables future autostart and\npreserves binaries, configuration, logs, and provider credentials.\n\n### Scenario catalog (`tests/lifecycle/scenarios/`)\n\n| Scenario | Asserts |\n|---|---|\n| `end-to-end-events` | Snapshot bootstrap + tail + event ordering |\n| `sse-resume` | `Last-Event-ID` replays the gap after a mid-stream drop |\n| `restart-resync` | `stream.resync_required` fires after a server restart |\n| `chaos-reconnect-storm` | Seeded high-drop-rate chaos doesn't lose events |\n| `plan-review-blocked` | Leftover-state throws → 409 + named events on the wire |\n| `delete-conversation-fk` | FK fail → 409 + named events, not a raw 500 |\n| `recovery-exhaustion` | Cap reached → `recovery.gave_up` + `error.surfaced` in order |\n| `worker-reattach` | Observer revival closes orphaned human-input requests, emits `worker.human_input_reconciled`, then emits `worker.reattached` |\n| `session-types` | Direct / planning / implementation conversation creation |\n| `conversation-continuation` | Mid-conversation SSE drop + reconnect; nothing lost |\n| `plan-improvement-flow` | Review start + worker spawn + restart-resync end-to-end |\n| `real-restart` | **Real subprocess** kill+respawn: sqlite persists, ring resets, client gets `stream.resync_required` |\n| `direct-mode-rerun` | User pressing \"re-run\" — same content sent twice persists as two rows in order |\n| `flaky-network` | Seeded HTTP flake: server stays consistent, deterministic across seed |\n| `worker-spawn-failure` | Bridge `spawnAgent` rejection now emits `error.surfaced(worker.spawn.failed)` instead of going silent |\n| `supervisor-stopped` | Every observer stop site emits `supervisor.stopped` with a typed reason (`run_terminated`, `run_failed`, `cwd_mismatch`, `snapshot_invalid`, `quota_exhausted`, `fatal_bridge_error`, `explicit`) |\n\n### Real protocol bug found via the harness\n\nWhile wiring `recovery-exhaustion`, the harness caught a real bug in the\nSSE route: when an `update` (snapshot) frame was about to be written,\nthe route would record the snapshot marker (advancing the cursor) and\nset `lastDeliveredId = marker.id` directly. Any named event emitted\n*during* the snapshot build — id < marker.id — was then silently dropped\non subsequent drains, because the next drain queried\n`getNamedEventsSince(marker.id, ...)`.\n\nFix: drain once more *before* recording the marker, so the marker's id\nis monotonically ahead of all already-emitted named events. This was a\nlatent bug that would have caused intermittent missed UI updates under\nload; the chaos harness paid for itself on its first real run.\n"
